A probe shows that no covered path reaches mode `0xD`, and a headless 8000-frame run is byte-identical before and after. New `check_2c2b0` in `test_fight.c` covers the text form, the sprite form, the release order and the call from `0x274FC`. `check_33c18_callers_b` (h) now seeds row `0x1D` empty so the new call finds nothing there. 24 of 27 mutations fail the suite, and the three survivors are equivalent.

**Mode 5, the round start (record §48-U).** `game_frame`'s case 5 now runs
`0x25C88` (`game_mode_05_step`), so 35 of the switch's entries remain named
gaps. It is not an arena-frame tail like modes `0xD`/`0x32` and shares no
code with them beyond the badge spawn `0x1D838`. It is a sub-state machine on
`DS_00104B25`. State 1 spawns the HUD (`0x25C1C`: `0x1D890`, or `0x1DC6C`
in `DS_00104B1D` mode 2, with the bars drawn at 0 by `0x1D2F0`/`0x1D464`,
then the per-round reset `0x20EF8`), the round card and the round-win
markers (`0x256F4`). State 2 spawns the fight card. State 4 holds each for
0x3C frames. State 3 releases both, draws the timer field (`0x4F37C`),
takes the play-time snaps (`0x32B00`/`0x32B4C`) and goes to mode 6, or to
mode `0xC` after the next side's entrance and badge. All of these callees
are ported from the raw, the `EAX != 0` arm of `0x1D890` included, which
was a §10.6 gap. §48-Q's `0x1D2F0` gap is closed and wired into `0x1D764`.
The entrance at `0x25F27` goes through `fn_resolve` as modes `0xD`/`0x32`
do, and six of the seven entrances are still gaps. Mode 5 arrives only
through the return mode `DS_00104AFA` (the ported hooks `0x259CC`/`0x27134`,
and the unported `0x28468`). A probe found the mode dword at 3 on every
frame of a headless 8000-frame run, and that run is byte-identical to
`main`'s. Named gaps: the voice, `0x32970` and the `0x2E934` audit, mode
`0x30`'s `0x29328` (the other caller of `0x25C1C`/`0x256F4`/`0x4F37C`),
`0x1D540`, `0x26A50` and `0x27DC8`. New `check_mode5_a`/`_b` in
`test_fight.c`: 47 of 48 mutations fail the suite. The survivor, `0x1D2F0`'s clamp at 0x77, is equivalent on the image tables (§48-U.4). `make verify` is green with the oracle lines unchanged.
A pre-existing flake came to light in this batch: `run_tests` dies with
SIGBUS in about one run in six, on `main`'s build as well. The crash is in
`check_projectile_step`'s `fighter_18350(side 1, anchor 0xFFFFF120)`, an
out-of-bounds table read. It is named in §48-U.4 and not fixed here.

**The join poll `0x28CC8` and modes 6/`0xC` (record §48-J).** `0x28CC8` is ported from the raw as `flow_join_poll`, and `game_frame` now dispatches cases 6 and `0xC` to it, so `flow_player_join` (`0x28DA4`, §48-Q) is finally wired. Case 6 polls only with `DS_00104B1D == 0`. The first side whose bit is clear in `DS_00104B1F` gets "INSERT 1 COIN" (no credit) or "PRESS START" (the sprite prompt at y `0x3A00` with the `DS_00104529` bit 1) at row `0x1D`, or, on its newly pressed start mask `0x9ACBC[side]`, joins. A join now also erases the side's prompt through `0x2C2B0` (`prompt_side_erase`, record §48-T, wired at merge time since that batch landed first) before storing the joined bit. A raw detail the tests pin: the join stores the side's bit before its `0x2CA7C(1)` spend, and `0x2CA7C` debits only while `DS_00104B1F` is 0 (the same guard `0x2CA48` uses), so a mid-match join needs a credit but never spends one. Named gap: case 6's no-join fall-back to case 4's fight frame `0x26254`. (Corrected by record §48-C: case `0xC`'s no-join arena frame `0x27380` is now ported and wired. Of the three callees this entry listed as unported, `0x3CB68` had been ported all along — `fight_slot_pass`, since the demo-fight arena frame `0x263F4` — and `0x1DA08`/`0x272DC` are ported by §48-C.) Every store to the mode word was decoded. Modes 6/`0xC` are now reachable under real input, since mode 5 (`0x25C88`, record §48-U, merged separately) stores them; the other stores are the unported `0x4CD98`, `0x4F0FC` and `0x2791C`, and `0x274FC` (mode `0xD`). A probe of the 8000-frame check still sees mode 3 alone (no covered path presses a real join), and the run is byte-identical before and after. New `check_join_poll` in `test_fight.c`: all 36 mutations fail the suite (36 real; the tests were re-checked at merge time against the row-release fix — the sentinel `DS_00105BF8 = 0xDEAD` had to become a realistic cell count once the eraser was wired, or the release walked past the text grid).

**The other six per-character entrances (record §48-V).** All seven entries of `0xA8628` are now ported, registered and unit-tested. The table is seven dwords with stride 4, from the dispatchers' `call [reg*4 + 0xA8628]`. The next dword starts the update table, so there is no secret-character entry. The six new ones are `0x40CB0` (character 0), `0x49150` (2), `0x15A34` (3), `0x45FE8` (4), `0x40E64` (5) and `0x24804` (6). Each is a separate copy of `0x24568`'s shape with its own constants, so each is its own C function: a placement offset of `0x3000` or `0x5000`, a stream, a blink byte `DS_00104B02 + c`, and state stores that differ per character. Character 0's entrance passes y = `0x4C00` to the spawn core `0x33C78`, which now threads EBX through. Each entrance stream is a sprite, a hold and a `0xD500` target that puts the fighter into its entrance pose. The targets are `0x40C34`, `0x492A8`, `0x159A8`, `0x46138`, `0x40E14` and `0x24964`, and they are ported with their callees `0x488B8`, `0x45878` (already ported, §48-R), `0x3DFC0` and `0x23530`. Character 6's entrance also calls `0x24754`, which fires a type-5 actor at the opponent. `0x488B8` and `0x23530` are also character 2's reaction-`0x22` and character 6's reaction-`0x24` callbacks, and are registered as such. That is 16 functions and 3275 raw bytes. Still named gaps: the voices. Mode 5 (§48-U, merged separately) does store mode `0xD`/`0x32`'s successors, so a headless 8000-frame run is checked against that merged state too, and is still byte-identical to the base. A headless 8000-frame run and the front-end driver's dump (the demo fight and the attract's second cycle) are byte-identical to the base. On the way, a pre-existing defect is fixed: `0x18350` forms its table address in EAX, so it wraps at 32 bits, but the port stepped about 4 GB past `mem` on a stale negative anchor. That made `check_projectile_step` die intermittently with SIGBUS (2 of 30 runs on `main`, 0 of 30 after the fix). New `check_char0/2/3/4/5/6_entrance` in `test_fight.c` (278 assertion sites), plus a wrapped-anchor row in `check_slot_latch` (the original row was vacuous — the anchor's `* 2u` already wrapped before the fix, so it never exercised the bug; replaced with an anchor whose product carries, per the independent review). Of 280 mutations, 254 fail an assertion, 18 spin the placement loop, 7 survive as equivalent and 1 cannot be told apart by the fixture (§48-V.4).
**Mode `0xC`'s arena frame `0x27380` (record §48-C).** `0x27380` is ported from the raw as `game_mode_0c_step`, and `game_frame`'s case `0xC` now calls it when the join poll finds no join (`0x2535D`), replacing the §48-J `PORT:` note. It first undoes the mode-`0xC` tail's blink (`0x25487`): with the `DS_00104B12` slot's `+0x41` bit 0 set and `+0x42` bit 3 clear, a pset word still at `0x1E1` (bit 15 masked) gets `DS_00104AF6` back whole. Then it runs `0x263F4`'s steps without `0x49C78`/`0x1282C`, and without `0x263F4`'s third `0x17FA0` pair. The projection block (`0x17FA0` x2, `0x17580`, `0x1958C`, `0x19068(0)`, `0x17FA0` x2, `0x1975C`) runs only while the byte `DS_001078FA` is 2. After it come `0x3CB68`, `0x35658` per side, `0x12DA8`, the new `0x1DA08`, the new `0x272DC` and `DS_00104AEC |= 2`. `0x1DA08` (`fight_hud_pulse`) counts down `DS_00102908[side]`; at 0 or below it restarts the `DS_001028F8` record's stream `0xE9050` at 4.0 and reloads `max((0x78 - +0x5A) >> 1, 0xC)`. `0x272DC` (`flow_arena_ko_check`) checks the `+0x5A` bytes against 0x78. If the side `(s8)DS_0010810D` reaches it, `0x27254` (`flow_match_snapshot`) runs: both slots and records are copied to `0x104890`/`0x1049B8` and the records' motion zeroed through the now-exported `0x33ACC`/`0x3C16C`/`0x3C148`. Then `0x278B0` (`flow_continue_open`) draws "TO CONTINUE THE FINAL BATTLE" and the countdown 15 through the new `0x2F434` (`text_number_set`, `0x2F4D0` without the cursor save) and stores mode `0xE`. If instead the `DS_00104B12` side reaches it, the other side's `+0x41` bit 4 is set and the mode becomes `0xD`. Scope corrections (the raw wins): `0x3CB68` was never a gap (it has been `fight_slot_pass` since the demo arena frame), `0x1DA08` was not ported by §48-J, and `0x272DC` brought `0x27254`, `0x278B0` and `0x2F434` with it. New `check_mode_0c` in `test_fight.c`, plus two pulse-word checks in `check_join_poll`. Of 86 mutations, 80 fail the suite and 6 are equivalent (each argued in §48-C.4). Named gaps: mode `0xE`'s `0x27A2C`, the `0x2C3FC` voices (§45-A), and the other callers of `0x1DA08`/`0x2F434`. No no-input path stores mode `0xC`: a probe of the 8000-frame check still sees mode 3 alone, and the run is byte-identical before and after. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained, `symbols.h` idempotent).
**Mode `0xE`'s continue screen `0x27A2C` (record §48-E).** `0x27A2C` is ported from the raw as `game_mode_0e_step`, and `game_frame`'s case `0xE` now calls it (`0x25371`). This is the screen that `0x278B0` (§48-C) opens. The side `w = (s8)DS_0010810D` takes the continue when the new `0x42F60` (`flow_continue_poll`) finds a credit and `w`'s start mask `0x9ACBC[w]` newly pressed. It spends one credit through `0x2CA7C(1)` (before `DS_00104B1F` is set, so it debits) and sets `DS_00105C04`. Then `DS_00104B1F = w + 1`, the new `0x2791C` (`flow_continue_take`) and `0x41310(w, 1)`. `0x2791C` erases rows `0xA`, `0xC`, `0xE` and `0xF`, restores both `0x27254` snapshots through `0x33B00`, and runs `0x1D764` on `w`. It then sets `w`'s `+0x5B = DS_00104B0B`, runs `0x46534(DS_00104B12, -2)`, and stores mode `0xC` with `w`'s `+0x41 &= 0xE7`. Otherwise the countdown `DS_00108110` ticks on every frame word with its low six bits 0, or on a forced tick. `DS_00105C04` forces one and restarts the count at `0xA`. A newly pressed button forces one: through `0x4F778(1)` with the `DS_00104B1F` bit 0, else `0x4F778(0)` with bit 1. Each tick redraws the count on row `0xE`. A tick below 0 runs `0x29B74` instead (darken all, mode `0x15`), with `DS_00104B25 = 0` and `DS_00104AFA = 0x1E`. While the signed count is below `0xE`, row `0xC` blinks "PRESS START" (with a credit) or "INSERT 1 COIN". Then `DS_00104AEC |= 2`. Scope (the raw wins): `0x2791C` and `0x42F60` were real gaps. `0x42F60` is not `0x11F28`, though it looks alike. Its spend goes through `0x2CA78` (`config_credit_zero`, `xor eax,eax; ret`, new here), so its `0x2CA48` arm is dead. The other nine callees were already ported. `0x2DAE4(0x11, 1)` (spec §7) and the two `0x2C3FC` voices (§45-A) are deferred `PORT:` notes. New `check_mode_0e` in `test_fight.c`. Of 70 mutations, 69 fail the suite. The one survivor swaps in the dead arm, which is equivalent under the credit precondition (§48-E.4). Named gaps: `0x42F60`'s other caller `0x42CB4` (since ported, record §48-D), the voices and the audit. Mode `0x15`'s `0x4F24C` is since ported (record §48-X). No no-input path reaches mode `0xE`, and a `--check 8000` run is byte-identical before and after. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained, `symbols.h` idempotent).
**The fight frame `0x26254`, modes 4 and 6 (record §48-K).** `0x26254` is ported from the raw as `game_mode_04_step`, and `game_frame` now dispatches case 4 (`0x25242`) and case 6's no-join fall-back (`0x2525D`/`0x25266`, which jump to that same entry) to it. It is the arena frame `0x263F4` with the projection block gated on `DS_001078FA == 2`, plus a tail: the HUD pulse countdowns `0x1DA08`, the round-end check `0x27FA8`, `DS_00104AEC |= 2`, and the mode-`0x25` entry `0x4E11C` behind `DS_001078FA == 2`, the word `DS_00108892 >= 6` and slot 0's `+0x53 == 0`. Its whole unported tree came with it (14 functions): `0x27FA8` ends a round when a `+0x5A` byte reaches `0x78` or the timer byte `DS_001088F2` runs out, through the winner `0x27C48` (counts, the `+0x5B` store — the raw stores `0x78 - A` for either winner —, the bonuses `0x25FDC`/`0x2604C`, `0x256F4`, the result `0x27BA4`), then either the round over (`0x39FF4` freezes both fighters, `0x27ED8` sets mode `0xA`; its `0x78 - max(+0x5A)` divisor is always `0x78` because `0x1D764` zeroed both bytes first) or the match's end (`0x27DC8`, then mode 9/8, or 7 on a timer-out); `0x4E11C` with `0x4E350`/`0x4E27C` spawns the ten mode-`0x25` entries; `0x34038` (the stun end), `0x38154` (the mode-`0x25` approach step) and `0x19820`. The named-gap call sites `0x357EE` (`0x34038` in `0x35658`), `0x366FB` and `0x3871F` (`0x38154`) are wired. Corrections: `0x27BA4` writes `DS_00108860[0..1]`, not `DS_00108864` as Ghidra's xref and `0x4AC80`'s note said; `check_join_poll`'s case-6 rows asserted the old gap and now check that the fight frame runs (every old assertion kept). `0x1DA08` is `fight_hud_pulse`, the name and body of the parallel `gap14-27380` port, so the merge keeps one copy. Named gaps: case `0xB`'s second call, `0x28C38`, is since ported (record §48-B); entries 15/16 of the per-frame update table `DS_000A8644` (dispatched every frame at `0x24CEF`) — `0x260BC` (entry 15, calling `0x2604C`) and its twin `0x26194` (entry 16) — which the newly ported `0x25FDC`/`0x2604C` switch on via `+0x8` bits `0x80`/`1` but neither is ported, so the port silently skips both every frame (inert: nothing sets those bits before this batch, and no oracle path reaches a round end); the other callers of the new functions; the voices; the run clock and audit adds. Probes of `--check 8000` and the front-end driver run see mode 3 alone, no fight frame and no `0x357EE` stun end, and the 8000-frame run is byte-identical to `main`'s; `make verify`'s lines are unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight exact at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent). New `check_fight_frame_a/b/c` in `test_fight.c`: 228 of 231 mutations fail the suite, and the 3 survivors are equivalent (`0x27ED8`'s dead maximum, twice, and `0x1DA08`'s `0xC` floor rewritten with `0xC`). See §48-K of the record.
**The entrance poses' slot callbacks (record §48-P).** The slot callbacks named as gaps in §48-V are now ported, registered and unit-tested, along with every callee no ported code had. That is 21 functions and 3271 raw bytes. The three chains store three callbacks each: character 0's `0x3F360`/`0x3F1F0`/`0x3F284`, character 2's `0x487D4`/`0x48668`/`0x486F8` and character 6's `0x233A8`/`0x23250`/`0x232B4`, in the slot's `+0x0C`/`+0x18`/`+0x1C`. The fight machinery calls them from `0x3531C` case 7, `0x19020` and `0x193B0`'s `0x19505`. The new callees are `0x3F184`, `0x3F308`, `0x13244`, `0x3C404` and `0x3605C`. `0x3F3F4` is character 0's reaction-`0x26`/`0x27` callback (rows `0xA3820`/`0xA3834`), and it installs `0x40C34`'s three callbacks. `0x3DE54` is installed by `0x3E064`, character 5's reaction-`0x22` callback (`0xA50D0`), which was unported too. So `0x3E064` is ported with its other callbacks `0x3DD14`/`0x3DD84`, and so are the other slot's `0x3D424` (`+0x10`) and `0x3D3E4` (`+0x14`), which `0x3DD84` stores. The Ghidra MCP did not connect, but its HTTP instance on `127.0.0.1:8089` did, and its decompilations agree with capstone over the fixed-up image. Two of them fold globals into constants, and the port reads the globals. The record corrects §48-V's size for `0x3F284` (130 bytes, not 219). The headless 8000-frame run and the front-end determinism dump are byte-identical to `main`, so the two newly live reaction callbacks are not reached on the oracle paths — but they are the faithful behavior under real input, and a credited match reaching mode `0xC` with characters 0, 2, 5 or 6 (increasingly reachable now that mode 4/6's fight frame is wired, §48-K) will run them for real, with no oracle coverage. The oracles are unchanged: 517/801/3/2, N = 1886 and N = 3617. New `check_sc_char0/2/6/5` in `test_fight.c` have 325 assertion sites. The `0x3B714` arms are checked differentially against a reference run of the specified callees. Of 302 mutations, 296 fail an assertion. Five survivors are equivalent and one cannot be told apart by the fixture (§48-P.4). Still named gaps: the voices, `0x3F360`'s `0x62003` arm, and the unported other callers `0x3E6A8`, `0x3F450`, `0x21EA4`, `0x3D4DC` and `0x3D8AC`.

**Mode `0x15`'s countdown `0x4F24C` (record §48-X).** `0x4F24C` is ported from the raw as `frontend_mode_15_step`, and `game_frame`'s case `0x15` now calls it (`0x253E0`). It is `0x4F318`'s countdown (mode `0x17`, record §46-G) byte for byte up to the expiry, minus that handler's `DS_001088EE = 0xFFFF` store and its `DS_00104AE4` hook call: while `DS_001088EE` is non-zero it is decremented; at 0 the skip test `0x4F790` runs (2 zeroes `DS_00104AFE`, 1 takes `0x3C` off it). Then `DS_00104AFE` is decremented, and when its old value was `<= 0` (signed) `DS_00104B00` takes the return mode `DS_00104AFA`. The mode's two raw stores are `0x29B74` (mode `0xE`'s expiry, record §48-E: `DS_001088EE = DS_00104AFE = 0x78`, then `DS_00104AFA = 0x1E`) and `0x41578` (record §42-E: `DS_00104AFE = 0x78`, `DS_001088EE = 0`, `DS_00104AFA = 0x13`). New `check_mode_15` in `test_fight.c`: direct steps through every branch (the countdown, both skip-test results, the signed-boundary cases at 0/1/`0x7FFF`/`0x8000`) plus an end-to-end run from `0x27A2C`'s expiry through 0x78 steps to the mode change, confirming the two countdowns and the return-mode dispatch line up with §48-E's own account. No no-input path reaches mode `0x15`, and `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained, `symbols.h` idempotent).
**Mode `0xB`'s winner-pose tick `0x28C38` (record §48-B).** `0x28C38` is ported from the raw as `flow_winner_pose_step`, and `game_frame`'s case `0xB` — until now entirely unwired, despite the fight frame `0x26254` (record §48-K) documenting what it calls — now runs `game_mode_04_step()` (the fight frame) then `flow_winner_pose_step()` (`0x25287`/`0x2528C`, confirmed against the raw bytes). The running count `DS_00104AD8` (reset to 0 at `0x25C1C`, record §48-U) is incremented and taken modulo `DS_00104AA8` (the duration `flow_round_over` loads, record §48-K); a non-zero signed remainder returns at once. Otherwise, per side, while its `+0x5A` byte is below `0x77`, `0x392A0`(slot, 1, 0) nudges its win pose — `fighter_392a0`, already ported for two other callers, now exported for this one. New `check_mode_b` in `test_fight.c`: the modulo gate (a non-firing remainder leaves both sides untouched), the per-side threshold (both below `0x77` nudge by exactly 1; at `0x77` a side is left alone, tested both orderings), and a `game_frame` dispatch check that a non-firing tick leaves `+0x5A` exactly as `0x26254` left it. Verified against a temporary mutation of the modulo gate (forced always-open), which the tests catch. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` green x3, no SIGBUS. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained, `symbols.h` idempotent). No no-input path reaches mode `0xB`.

**Mode `0x13`'s challenge screen `0x424E8` (record §48-D).** `0x424E8` is ported from the raw as `game_mode_13_step`, and `game_frame`'s case `0x13` now calls it (`0x253C4`). It is the screen after a match where the loser (either side on a draw) can press start to challenge. Mode `0x13` is the return mode that mode `0x15` takes after mode 9's `0x28788` (three sites, through mode `0x17`'s hook `0x29B74`) or `0x41578`, each with `DS_00104B25 = 0`. A six-entry jump table (`0x424D0`) steps `DS_00104B25`. Sub-state 0 resets the actors, spawns the `0xC87BC` row and runs the new `0x428B8` (`flow_challenge_open`). That sets the count to 9, drops two records from y `-0x3C00`, and, on the match result `DS_00104AD4`, spawns the loser's prompt, `0xC838C` and count children. `0xC83B4`'s `+0x10` handle is patched by the new `0x29CBC` first. Sub-state 1 is the new `0x42BCC` (`flow_challenge_drop`): gravity and a `-(v / 3)` bounce until both records land. Sub-state 2 clears the effects and runs the new `0x20E90` (`flow_scroll_reset`), the new `0x42724` (`flow_challenge_fighters`: both slot records respawned from the result's descriptors, with palettes and secondary actors), the new `0x4B9AC` (`fight_challenge_crowd`, `0x494A8`'s sibling: up to 10 fight-effect entries per side), and a palette fade on every list entry except handle `0x3E708` and `0x1C6D4`'s nine. Sub-state 3 waits for no active effect. Sub-state 4 runs the new `0x42CB4` (`flow_challenge_poll`) and the health bars. On the loser's credited start (`0x42F60`) that takes the challenge through the new `0x42FE0` (`flow_challenge_join`), the hook `0x28D68` and sub-state 5. Otherwise the count ticks, and below 0 the mode becomes `0x1E` (or `0x17` with the hook `0x25AE8`). Sub-state 5 runs `0x4F318`, whose hook enters the character screen. After the switch, each side not skipped by `DS_00104B1D`'s rule gets "PRESS START" or "INSERT 1 COIN". `0x29BC8` (`fighter_29bc8`) is exported for `0x42724`. Raw quirks kept, not fixed: in `0x42724`'s result-1 arm, side 1's a3 is `0xC834C[c0]`, not `c1`'s (`ESI = c0 * 2`), and both secondary actors use `0xBB8D0[c0]` (a constant `[esp+4]`). With a result outside 0..2, `0x428B8`'s tail writes to whatever `DS_001080F8` held. Deferred as `PORT:` notes: the `0x2C` and `0x2D` voices (§45-A) and the no-op call `0xC7F58[stage]` (both targets are bare returns, as in `0x412A0`). Of the 19 listed callees, 13 were already ported (`0x33904`, `0x29CFC` (a `jmp 0x13DF0`), `0x2C060`, `0x38B18`, `0x2BAF4`, `0x1C6D4`, `0x13C70`, `0x2C1C8`, `0x2C178`, `0x4F318`, `0x33F08`, `0x4F1D0`, `0x2C3FC`) and six were not. With their unported callees `0x29CBC` and `0x42FE0`, eight functions are new. New `check_mode_13` in `test_fight.c` (callees directly, the dispatch, the tail and `game_frame`'s case). The crowd is checked entry by entry against replayed rng draws, with seeds that land on both lane-0 bounds exactly. 15 of 15 mutations fail the suite. At the time this record was written, no ported path reached mode `0x13` (`0x28788`, `0x416D4` and `0x41C28` were unported — `0x28788` is now ported, record §48-Y). `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent).

**Mode 9's frame handler `0x28788` (record §48-Y).** `0x28788` is ported from the raw as `game_mode_09_step`, and `game_frame`'s case 9 — until now a named gap — now calls it (`0x2533F`). It is the post-match results frame: unlike every other mode handler ported so far, it carries no sub-state jump table of its own. Its preamble is a slimmer, *unconditional* sibling of the fight frame `0x26254` (record §48-K) — `fight_slot_clear`, `camera_screen_base` per side, the two position latches, then (with no `DS_001078FA == 2` gate) `camera_project` per side, `camera_decay`, `fighter_pass_a`; `fight_slot_pass` only when neither side's `+0x41` byte has bit 1 set; then always `fight_hud_pass` per side, `fighter_pass_b(1)` (`0x26254`'s own gated block passes 0), `fight_effects_pass` and, of `camera_scene_step`'s pair, only `camera_y_commit`. A countdown word (`DS_00104AF8`) reaching 0 this frame arms two per-side flag bits and `DS_000F0AFE = 4`; `DS_00104AEC |= 2` always. Only with `DS_000F0AFE == 4` and `DS_001078FC != 0` does the results state run — and the countdown reaching 0 can open that gate in the very same call. It holds every effects-list entry (the new `fight_effects_hold_all`, `0x4A708`, fight.c), draws the match-result caption (`flow_match_result_text`, already ported) and updates the win-streak/handicap bookkeeping (the new static `flow_match_streak_update`, `0x286BC`, flow.c, whose own callee `0x4660C` is ported as the new `fighter_4660c`, fighter.c). It rearms the front-end countdowns, optionally forces a draw (`DS_00108173`), optionally snapshots the play-time accumulator (`config_play_time_snap`, record §48-Q, already ported), then dispatches on the match result into one of four arms: a draw, a decided result whose winner is "thinking" and whose loser's win-streak crosses a threshold, the same but below threshold, and a decided result where the winner isn't thinking — split further on the stage word (7 clears the stage marks first via the already-ported `fight_stage_marks_clear`; any other stage only installs the mode-`0x12` hook `fight_hook_4142c`, with no return-mode store). Three of the four arms darken into mode `0x17` with return mode `0x13` — mode 9 is one of the two ported paths into `game_mode_13_step`'s challenge screen (record §48-D), which had no ported path in before this task. Four call sites inside `0x28788` (`0x289D9`, `0x28AFF`, `0x28B25`, `0x28BA2`) target `0x32BAC`, which `read_memory` and Ghidra's own function table both show is a bare `ret` — the tail instruction of the unrelated `FUN_00032B94`, not a function of its own — so a `CALL` there is a proven no-op (`get_xrefs_to` lists eight such call sites across the whole image, not only this function's four); the port omits the register-only computations that fed them. New `check_mode_09` in `test_fight.c`, three parts: a direct unit of `fight_effects_hold_all` (`mz_seed`'s per-entry fixture), the preamble/gates (`check_mode_09_a`), and the results state's four arms plus `flow_match_streak_update`/`fighter_4660c`'s own effects, including a dedicated case that pins the streak counter to force `fighter_4660c`'s clamp (`check_mode_09_b`). The fixture is `tf_demo_fixture`, not `q_mode_seed`, because `0x28788`'s preamble calls `camera_project` and `fight_slot_pass` unconditionally, which `q_mode_seed` is deliberately built to let `0x26254`'s tests skip; two real fixture gaps this task found (the DPMI selector base `hit_frame_desc` falls back to, and the per-side character byte its table row is keyed on — both addresses this function's own logic also reads/writes) are now seeded. 8 of 8 mutations fail the suite. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` green x5, no SIGBUS. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — mode 9 is reachable only through the still-unported match-end stores (`0x27DC8`, record §48-K) and the coin/start chain, so the no-input oracle path never leaves mode 3. Still named gaps: mode 8's `0x28468` (the other `0x4A708`-shaped caller) and mode `0xA`'s `0x28BD4`.

**Mode 8's frame handler `0x28468` (record §49-C).** `0x28468` is ported from the raw as `game_mode_08_step`, and `game_frame`'s case 8 — until now a named gap — now calls it (`0x25335`). It is a near-identical, but considerably slimmer, sibling of mode 9's `0x28788` (`game_mode_09_step`, record §48-Y, whose own named-gap note led into this task). The preamble is instruction-for-instruction the same as mode 9's: `fight_slot_clear`, `camera_screen_base` per side, the two position latches, then unconditionally `camera_project` per side, `camera_decay`, `fighter_pass_a`. The one structural difference: `fight_slot_pass` runs here with no gate at all, where mode 9 gates it on neither side's `+0x41` byte having bit 1 set. Then, again identically to mode 9, `fight_hud_pass` per side, `fighter_pass_b(1)`, `fight_effects_pass` and only `camera_y_commit` of `camera_scene_step`'s pair. The countdown word `DS_00104AF8` counts down the same way, arming `DS_001078FE`/`DS_001078FC = 1`, OR-ing 0x10 into both sides' `+0x41` byte and setting `DS_000F0AFE = 4` on reaching 0; either way `DS_00104AEC |= 2`. The results-state gate is the other structural difference: it reads `DS_001078FE` (not mode 9's `DS_001078FC`) then `DS_000F0AFE`, the same pair of conditions by outcome (both bytes are always set together when the countdown arms) but a literally different byte and test order, ported exactly as disassembled rather than copied from mode 9. The results state itself is far slimmer than mode 9's: it holds every effects-list entry and draws the match-result caption through `fight_effects_hold_all` (`0x4A708`) and `flow_match_result_text` (`0x28130`) — both leads from mode 9's own port turned out to be the *same* functions mode 9 calls, not separate near-identical siblings, confirmed by `get_xrefs_to 0x4A708` listing both call sites — so no new callee was needed at all. Mode 8 never reads or writes the match result `DS_00104AD4`: no win/lose/draw dispatch, no streak update, no `DS_00108173` draw force, no play-time snapshot/close. It only rearms the front-end countdowns (`DS_00104AFE = 0xF0`, `DS_001088EE = 0x3C`, the same constants mode 9 uses) and, on the byte `DS_00104B1D`, unconditionally installs the already-ported hook `game_hook_25bbc` (`DS_00104AE4 = 0x25BBC`, record §46-B — the same hook `0x259CC` installs), sets `DS_00104B25 = 1` and mode `DS_00104B00 = 0x16`; only the return mode `DS_00104AFA` differs, `0x30` when `DS_00104B1D == 3` else 5. No call site in `0x28468` targets the `0x32BAC` bare-`ret` stub mode 9's port found four of; all fifteen `CALL` instructions in this function's 100-instruction body were checked and every one targets a real, already-ported callee. New `check_mode_08` in `test_fight.c`, two parts on a new `m08_seed` fixture (a close sibling of mode 9's `m09_seed`, needed because mode 8's `fight_slot_pass` is unconditional on every call, not just one branch): `check_mode_08_a` proves the preamble runs `fight_slot_pass` even with both sides' gate bit set (the condition that would suppress it in mode 9) and exercises the results-gate's two conditions independently and together; `check_mode_08_b` proves both `DS_00104B1D` arms' return modes and that mode 8's results state leaves `DS_00104AD4`/`DS_00107813`/`DS_001078A7`/`DS_00108173`/`DS_0010746C` untouched at their sentinels. Three single-site mutations (the slot-pass gate reinstated, the results-gate byte swapped to `DS_001078FC`, and the two return-mode constants swapped) each fail the suite (12 `CHECK_EQ_INT` failures total) and were reverted. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` green x3, no SIGBUS. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — mode 8 is reachable only through the same still-unported match-end stores (`0x27DC8`, record §48-K) that leave mode 9 unreached, so the no-input oracle path never leaves mode 3. Still named gaps: mode `0xA`'s `0x28BD4`, the match-end stores `0x27DC8`, and the dead code region at `0x2861C` (immediately after `0x28468`'s own `ret`, storing `0x29B74` but reachable from nowhere in the image).

**Mode `0xA`'s frame handler `0x28BD4` (record §49-D).** `0x28BD4` is ported from the raw as `game_mode_0a_step`, and `game_frame`'s case `0xA` — until now grouped with modes 7/8/9 in the generic named-gap list — now calls it (`0x2527D`). Confirmed as case `0xA` two ways: the decompiled `switch(DAT_00104b00)` at `0x24C5C` has `case 10: ... FUN_00028bd4(); break;`, and `get_xrefs_to 0x28BD4` lists exactly that one call site with no register setup before the bare `CALL` — the bare-dispatch-stub shape every case shares. Despite sharing modes 8/9's named-gap listing line, it is **not a third results-screen sibling**: at only 100 bytes / 23 instructions, it is the *tail* render/commit slice those two share — the two position latches, `fight_hud_pass` per side, `fighter_pass_b(1)`, `fight_effects_pass` and `camera_y_commit` — with none of their `fight_slot_clear`/`camera_screen_base`/`camera_project`/`camera_decay`/`fighter_pass_a` preamble, no `DS_00104AF8` countdown and no match-result dispatch. `flow.h`'s existing mode-4 derivation already named `0x27ED8` "(mode `0xA`)" as the round-over path (against `0x27DC8`'s match-over path into modes 9/8/7); this task confirms mode `0xA` is a short wait state between a round ending and mode `0xB`'s winner-pose tick. Once both sides' slot `+0x54` byte (`DS_00107804`/`DS_00107898` — the same field the `0xD500` animation opcode clears, and mode 7's `0x282C4` also reads, against a different threshold) read zero, it plays voice `0xD8` and advances the mode word unconditionally to `0xB`. That last step required tracing `0x2C3FC`'s own disassembly by hand: `EDX = 0xB` is loaded before the call, and every one of `0x2C3FC`'s ~20 exit paths ends `POP EDI; POP EDX; POP EBX; RET`, restoring the caller's original `EDX` regardless of the voice's own outcome — so `DS_00104B00 = DX` at `0x28C2F` (Ghidra's `extraout_DX`) is unconditionally `0xB`, once the gate opens, matching the codebase's own existing `0x2C3FC`-preserves-`EDX` precedent (`frontend_char_screen_hook_voice`'s header comment, record §42-E.2). No new callee: all five non-voice calls are the same already-ported functions modes 8/9 call, and the voice call (`0x2C3FC`) is the established named gap (record §45-A), left as a `PORT:` note per spec §7. New `check_mode_0a` in `test_fight.c` on a new `m0a_seed` fixture (`tf_demo_fixture` plus mode-`0xA` seeding — no hitbox-array setup needed, unlike modes 8/9's fixtures, since this function never calls `fight_slot_pass`/`camera_project`): the position latches, `camera_y_commit`'s tail store into `DS_001078F2`'s high word (proven with distinct `DS_001077E0`/`DS_00107874` seeds), and the gate's word-only mode store, tested with both sides zero (advances) and each side individually nonzero (blocks), against a `0xBEEF`-high-word sentinel proving only the low word moves. Two single-site mutations (removing the latches/`camera_y_commit` call; widening the gate to accept side 0 nonzero) each fail the suite (3 and 2 `CHECK_EQ_INT` failures respectively) and were reverted. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` green x3 (plus the 2 mutation-revert runs), no SIGBUS. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — mode `0xA` is reachable only through the round-end path inside the still-unported fight frame `0x26254` (record §48-K), so the no-input oracle path never leaves mode 3. Still named gaps: the round-end path itself (`0x27FA8`/`0x27ED8`/`0x27DC8`, inside `0x26254`), mode 7's `0x282C4`, and the `0x2C3FC` voice dispatcher (record §45-A).
**Two live interactive-fight-path gaps: `fighter_pass_b`'s `0x1922C` skip and `hit_reaction_pick`'s `0x46460`/`0x4649C` scanner (record §49-B).** Unlike every other named-gap task in this record, no new function needed porting: `0x1922C` (`hit_stance_timer`) and `0x46460`/`0x4649C` (`fighter_input_read`/`fighter_input_scan`) were already fully ported and already called from other sites in `fighter.c` — the only defects were two call sites left as stale `PORT:`-commented stubs. `fighter_pass_b`'s stance-mismatch arm (`0x190C1`) now calls `hit_stance_timer(side)` before its `continue`, matching the raw's `MOV EAX,EDX; CALL 0x1922C`. `hit_reaction_pick`'s `slot+0x63 == 0` arm (`0x1DEDC`..`0x1DF3E`) now runs the raw's counted scan: `fighter_input_read(side, i)` for each ring index `i` in `[0, n)`; a word with no low-nibble bit set is skipped; otherwise two `fighter_input_scan` probes (`n1 = i, n2 = 3`, masks `3` and `0xC`) decide ambiguity — both hitting returns `0xFF` immediately (the function's own fallback), otherwise once `i >= 2` that word becomes the result and the scan stops, below that floor it keeps going. Both gaps are on the live interactive-fight path (gated on real input/stance state), not the no-input demo/attract oracle path, so `make verify`'s gates are unaffected by design and the new unit tests are the only correctness net. `fighter.h`'s stale "named gap (§7.6)" claim on `fighter_pass_b` is corrected — its other citee `0x3CF38` (`hit_chain_resolve`) had been fully ported all along, so the function is now "Fully ported"; `hit_reaction_pick`'s header is likewise corrected. `check_fighter_pass_b` (pre-existing) is extended: its second scenario had asserted the *stub's* no-op behavior, which is now corrected to `0x1922C`'s real post-condition (`DS_00100B5E`/`DS_00100B5A` clear unconditionally), and both scenarios gain side-1 coverage of the write and guard-false arms the stance-mismatch skip was already silently exercising on that side. New `check_hit_reaction_scan` proves the scanner's `n = 5`/`0xF` selection boundary, the no-bits skip, the below-floor continue, the ambiguous early return and the `i >= 2` capture, against a ring pinned at `DS_001082D2 = 0x000F0000` — the first direct exercise of `fighter_input_read`/`fighter_input_scan` by name anywhere in `test_fight.c`. Five mutations (the new `hit_stance_timer` call removed, the ambiguity check disabled, the capture floor weakened/strengthened by one, the `n`-boundary loosened) all fail the suite and were reverted byte-for-byte. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` green x3, no SIGBUS. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent). No functions remain to port for this record; the §7.6/§7.12 labels on `0x1922C`/`0x46460`/`0x4649C` are gone from `fighter.c`/`fighter.h` (the remaining §7.6/§7.12 mentions in `port/spec/game_flow.md` are historical narrative about other, unrelated addresses).

**Mode 7's frame handler `0x282C4` (record §49-E) and mode `0xF`'s `0x277C0` plus its idle-pose callee `0x4DEF4` (record §49-F).** Both are ported from the raw and wired: `game_frame`'s case 7 now calls the new `game_mode_07_step`, and case `0xF` the new `game_mode_0f_step` (`flow.c`); both case numbers were confirmed independently via `get_xrefs_to`, each showing exactly one bare-`CALL` dispatch-stub site (`0x25273`/`0x2537B`), the same proof method §49-D used for case `0xA`. Neither mode turned out to resemble modes 8/9 the way the pre-task lead suggested. Mode 7 has no `fight_slot_clear`/`camera_screen_base`/`camera_project`/`camera_decay`/`fighter_pass_a` preamble at all — just `fight_slot_pass` unconditionally, then the shared tail (the two position latches, `fight_hud_pass` per side, `fighter_pass_b(1)`, `fight_effects_pass`, `camera_y_commit`) — followed by a three-way dispatch on the round winner `DS_00104B16` (0/1 a side, 2 a draw) crossed with the match result `DS_00104AD4`, each arm testing a per-side "attack state" byte (the winning side's own slot `+0x52`, or the other/both sides' slot `+0x54` — the same field mode `0xA`'s port already named) before optionally arming `DS_00104AF8 = 0x258` and advancing to mode 8 or 9; every path ends `DS_00104AEC |= 2` and an unchanged `DS_00104AD4` reload/store-back. Mode `0xF` calls `fight_hud_pass` per side but not `fighter_pass_b`; in its place, a new callee `fight_effects_idle_pass` (`fight.c`/`fight.h`) walks the same `DS_0010884C` active-effects list `fight_effects_pass`/`fight_effects_hold_all` already walk, first rearming a background-flourish voice/countdown pair (`DS_001088B0`/`DS_001088BB`, the same pair `fight_4dbec`'s winner-crowd spawn also arms) with a real `rng_next(0x14) + 0x78` draw, then dispatching each list entry through a 5-state jump table whose states 1..4 all gate on `0x4A868` — which is **not a new gap this task introduces**: it is the exact case-13/14 named gap `fight_effects_pass`'s own header comment already left unported (spec §7.4, predating this task by several merges) — so the port treats that gate as always false, matching the precedent `fight_effects_pass` itself already set for the identical call; unlike that function's own case-13/14 stand-ins, no RNG-consumption stub is needed here since `0x4DEF4`'s four gated state bodies draw no RNG at all. Back in `game_mode_0f_step`, `DS_00104AEC |= 2` runs unconditionally and the word `DS_00104AFE` decrements every call with **no zero-guard** (unlike modes 8/9's `DS_00104AF8` countdown, which tests non-zero first); at or below zero it plays a deferred voice, clears a bit on the established winner-side macro `DS_0010810D`'s slot, awards `fighter_41310(DS_00104AD4, 0x30D40)`, runs the deferred audit `0x32B94` (disassembled in full: 8 instructions, a conditional `0x2DAE4` post then a bare `ret`, so a proven no-op either way) and the deferred run-clock `0x32970`, then `config_play_time_close(1, DS_00104B19)` — confirming the task brief's lead that this was one of that function's two remaining live, unported callers — and finally installs `frontend_darken_all` (`0x29B74`) under mode `0x17`, the one respect in which the pre-task lead's mode-9 comparison held. No new callees beyond `fight_effects_idle_pass` were needed for either mode; every other call in both functions targets an already-ported function. New `check_mode_07` (eight scenarios covering the winner/result three-way dispatch and its four advance/no-advance outcomes) and `check_mode_0f` (four scenarios covering the countdown's no-zero-guard behavior, including an already-zero-at-entry case, and the winner-side/match-result argument independence) in `test_fight.c`, registered right after `check_mode_0a`. Wiring case 7 broke a pre-existing assumption elsewhere: `test_game.c`'s `test_flow` used `DS_00104B00 = 7` as an arbitrary "any mode but 3" stand-in (case 7 used to be a no-op), and now that it runs the real fight-frame chain against that test's title-state fixture, `run_tests` hung at 100% CPU forever inside the already-ported `fight_4b69c` (the worshipper-trample pass, called through `fight_effects_pass`). `/usr/bin/sample` on the hung process pinned the exact call chain (`test_flow` -> `game_frame` -> `game_mode_07_step` -> `fight_effects_pass` -> `fight_4b69c`, looping); the fix retargets that test to `0x16` (still an inert named gap) with a comment explaining why 7 stopped being safe (record §49-E.5) — not a defect in the new port itself, but a real, repo-wide hazard of wiring a previously-inert case that this task's own verification loop caught before it reached `main`. Five mutations were actually built and run (not merely predicted): dropping mode 7's shared-tail `DS_00104AEC |= 2` (3 failures), narrowing its winner!=2 in-range test (1 failure), flipping its other-side selector (2 failures), reinstating mode 0xF's countdown zero-guard (1 failure), and swapping `fighter_41310`'s side argument for `DS_0010810D` in mode 0xF — this last one was **not caught on first try**, a genuine coverage gap (every other scenario coincidentally seeds `DS_0010810D` and `DS_00104AD4` to the same value), fixed by extending the winner-side scenario with a second scratch record and re-confirmed caught (2 failures) before reverting. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` green x3 (plus all mutation-revert runs), no SIGBUS. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — both modes are reachable only through the same still-unported match-end stores (`0x27DC8`, record §48-K) that leave modes 8/9 unreached, so the no-input oracle path never leaves mode 3. Still named gaps: `0x4A868` (spec §7.4), mode `0x33`'s `0x29638` (`0x4DEF4`'s other caller), the match-end stores `0x27DC8`, and the `0x2C3FC`/`0x2DAE4` voice/audit idiom (record §45-A, spec §7).

**Mode `0x12`'s post-match "next opponent" screen (record §48-Z).** `game_frame`'s case `0x12` now runs `0x41C28` (`game_mode_12_step`), so 27 of the switch's entries remain named gaps. `0x416D4`, previously listed alongside it as a second unported entry, turns out to be `0x41C28`'s own internal helper (its only caller is state 5, `0x420FE`) and is ported as the `static` `frontend_mode12_darken_or_tally`, not a second `game_frame` case. `0x41C28` is a nine-state machine on `DS_00104B25` (a jump table at `0x41C04`): a resumable one-slot-per-frame scan of the seven portrait actors for a newly-revealed opponent (state 1, tallying `DS_00108104` against each side's tagged character id), a "P1'S CHARACTER / VS / STAGE" banner once a background record scrolls past a fixed y (state 3), a flash-and-count pass over the current stage's portrait (states 4/5), a per-side scoreboard build with name/score text and a "confetti" scroll (state 5's `0x418F4`, `frontend_scoreboard_build`), a credits-reel scroll setup and playback (states 6/7), and a countdown (state 8) that is reused after nearly every other state. State 7's tail dispatches to a continue prompt (mode `0x17`, hook `game_hook_259cc`), a "no continue" screen (the new `flow_no_continue_screen`, `0x417C4`), or a join prompt (the new `flow_join_prompt_draw`, `0x271E0`) followed by the play-time audit close and a `longjmp` quit path (spec §7, deferred). Ten new leaf functions came with it: `flow_1082d0_from_column` (`0x4660c`, the continue-timer recompute `flow_1082c8_init` already reads column 0 of), `frontend_mode12_advance` (`0x4160c`), `frontend_mode12_challenge_continue` (`0x41760`), `frontend_portrait_flash`/`frontend_portrait_reveal` (`0x414B4`/`0x41528`), `flow_no_continue_screen`, `flow_join_prompt_draw`, `frontend_scoreboard_build`, and two small text-cursor wrappers in `actors.c` (`text_glyph_at`/`text_cursor_next_line`, `0x2F174`/`0x2F41C`). One genuinely dead call surfaced: `0x416D4`'s `0x32BAC` is a bare `RET` at its own entry byte (confirmed by `read_memory`), so all 8 of its call sites across the image — including this one — are no-ops; the port drops the call and its argument build with a cited `PORT:` note rather than porting a stub that does nothing. Named gaps: the `0x2C3FC` voices (§45-A) and the quit-path `longjmp` (spec §7). No no-input path reaches mode `0x12`. New `check_mode_12` in `test_fight.c` covers the four states self-contained enough to assert without actor/resource setup (the countdown, the out-of-range guard, the frame-level dispatch, and state 3's early-out); two source mutations (the countdown guard, the state-3 threshold) both fail the suite. A third mutation attempt — loosening the out-of-range guard alone — was found behaviourally inert, since a C `switch` with no matching case already no-ops for state 9+ regardless of the guard, unlike the raw's unconditional jump-table read; this is recorded rather than silently dropped. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` is green three times, no SIGBUS, and `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained, `symbols.h` idempotent).

**`fight_hud_pass`'s last two named gaps: the mode-4 respawn arm and the arena-wall clamp (record §49-A).** `fight_hud_pass` (`0x35658`) is fully ported: its two remaining named-gap call sites, `0x33C78` and `0x354F0`, are closed. The mode-4 arm (`0x35792`-`0x357DB`, gated on `DS_00104B00 == 4`, `rec+0x43` bit `0x80` clear and `DS_001088E0[side]` bit 0) turned out, on disassembly, to be a byte-identical duplicate of `fighter_spawn`'s own wrapper computation over `0x33C78` (`fighter_spawn_slot`) — a function already fully ported before this batch — so the port wires it by calling the existing `fighter_spawn(side)` rather than re-deriving those five register arguments a second time; either way the arm returns immediately once entered, confirming the previous stub's shape was already correct. The wall clamp `0x354F0` is ported as the new `fighter_wall_clamp(side)` in `fighter.c`, called for `side` then `1-side` from the pass's tail (`0x3581C`/`0x35824`). It clamps a fighter's `x` (slot `+0x2C`) to `+/-DS_000BE018` (31744, `0x7C00`) through the already-ported `hit_anchor_x`, and, when the clamped side is mid-hitstun (`+0x53 == 0xA`) and the other side is not blocking (`+0x54 != 2`) and the two fighters are close, drags the other side's slot by the same overshoot through `fighter_1883c` so it does not lag behind the clamp; if the clamped side is still driving into the wall its motion is zeroed via `fighter_3c148`. The "close" test's `ai_distance` (`0x187FC`) itself re-latches both slots before subtracting, which (since both slots' `+0x42` bit 3 gates `fighter_slot_latch` to take each record's own `+0x18` anchor) makes the comparison read the two fighter records' last-committed anchors rather than the just-clamped live position, and the same re-latch overwrites the clamp on the pushing side's own slot before the function returns — a raw-faithful quirk, confirmed from the disassembly after the new unit test's numbers first came out wrong assuming otherwise, and reproduced byte for byte rather than "fixed". New `check_wall_clamp` (nine scenarios: within-bounds no-op, the freeze gate, both walls' clamp with and without the velocity zero, and push-together on both walls with and without the distance gate) and `check_mode4_spawn_gate` (the bit-0 gate, set and clear) in `test_fight.c`, placed at the very end of `test_fight`'s call list rather than beside the other HUD-pass checks: `check_mode4_spawn_gate`'s `fighter_spawn` call touches the process-wide actor free list through `actors_reset()`/`actor_spawn`, and `check_point_trample` (much earlier in the list) reads a shadow's pset index off that free list's accumulated state — inserting the new checks in their natural position (right after `check_hud_latch`) made `check_point_trample` fail by shifting that index; moving them to the end, after everything that depends on the accumulation, fixed it with no other change. Verified by five temporary mutations (the freeze gate, the velocity-zero sign, the push delta's sign, and both directions of the mode-4 gate), each caught by the new tests and reverted. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` is green three times, no SIGBUS. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — expected, since both gaps are gated on live fight-mode/input state the no-input demo/attract/front-end paths never reach. This closes the `§7.8` gap `fight.h` named for `fight_hud_pass`.

**Mode `0x16`'s frame handler `0x4F2B0` (record §49-G).** `game_frame`'s case `0x16` now runs `0x4F2B0`, ported as `frontend_mode_16_step` (`flow.c`), confirmed as case `0x16` by `get_xrefs_to` (its one caller is `0x253E7`, `game_frame`'s own dispatch, at the jump-table slot between case `0x15`'s `0x4F24C` and case `0x17`'s `0x4F318`). It is the third member of a countdown family `flow.c`'s own pre-existing header comments on those two siblings already predicted: `0x4F24C`'s comment says "the body is `0x4F318`'s (and `0x4F2B0`'s) countdown byte for byte up to the expiry", and `0x4F318`'s says "the mode is left to the hook (unlike mode `0x16`'s `0x4F2B0`)". This task confirms both: `0x4F2B0` runs the identical `DS_001088EE`/`DS_00104AFE` countdown and `frontend_skip_check` skip test as its two siblings, but its expiry arm does *both* of what they each do separately — it runs the `DS_00104AE4` hook (as `0x4F318`/mode `0x17` does, with no `DS_001088EE = 0xFFFF` rearm) and then stores `DS_00104B00 = DS_00104AFA` (as `0x4F24C`/mode `0x15` does), hook first. No new callee: `frontend_skip_check` and the `fn_resolve` hook-call idiom are already ported and already used by `frontend_mode_17_step`. Reachable only through mode 8/9's results countdown (`game_mode_08_step`/`game_mode_09_step`, records §49-C/§48-Y, which install `game_hook_25bbc` and set mode `0x16` with return mode `0x30` or 5), itself gated behind the still-unported match-end stores `0x27DC8` (record §48-K), so the no-input oracle path never reaches it. New `check_mode_16` in `test_fight.c` (registered right after `check_mode_15`), with `m16_step`/`m16_fired` helpers modelled directly on the pre-existing `ms_step`/`ms_fired` (mode `0x17`) and `m15_step`/`m15_fired` (mode `0x15`) pairs: the shared countdown/skip-test boundary (a press taking `0x3C`, the `0x3D`/`0x3C` not-fired/fired split, a held mask firing at once, the signed `<= 0` boundary at `1`/`0`/`0x8000`/`0x7FFF`), that `DS_001088EE` is never rearmed to `0xFFFF` on a fire (unlike mode `0x17`), an unregistered hook skipped without crashing while the mode store still runs, and the load-bearing check that a fired step's final `DS_00104B00` is the *hook's own* `DS_00104AFA` write, not the seeded value — proving the raw's "hook first, mode store second" order, since the test's hook (`0x26978`/`0x26998`) itself writes `DS_00104AFA`. `check_mode_switch` gains a new (c2b) scenario proving `game_frame` itself routes mode `0x16` to the new handler and not the named-gap default. Three single-site mutations (swapping the hook/mode-store order, disabling `case 0x16u` in the switch, copying `0x4F318`'s `0xFFFF` rearm into `0x4F2B0`) each fail the suite and were reverted. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` green x3 (plus the 3 mutation-revert runs), no SIGBUS. `make verify` is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — expected, since mode `0x16` is unreachable on the no-input path for the same reason modes 8/9 are (record §48-K).
**Mode `0x1E`'s frame handler `0x1EEB0` (record §49-H).** `game_frame`'s case `0x1E` now runs `0x1EEB0` (`game_mode_1e_step`), the return mode mode `0x13`'s challenge poll takes when the post-match challenge window runs out unjoined. It turns out **not** to be simply "no continue → attract": it is a 17-state sub-machine on `DS_00104B25` (jump table `0x1EE6C`; state 1 and `AL > 0x10` both land on the shared tail `ret`, a genuine no-op). Five states are pure bookkeeping over already-ported callees and are fully wired: state 2 (reset, `frontend_match_start`, then `DS_00104AFE = 0x12C`/`DS_00104B00 = 0x15`/`DS_00104AFA = 0x1E`/`DS_00104B25 = 3`/`DS_001088EE = 0`); state 3 (reset, `DS_00104B25 = 0`, `DS_00104B00 = 0x14`); states 6/9 (reset, then — only when `DS_00104ABC == 1` — state 2's own quintet, before `DS_00104B25 = 7`/`0xA` unconditionally either way); and state 0xA (reset — whose `DS_00104B25 = 0` store survives `actors_reset`'s call by register-preservation proof, `0x2BAF4` pushes and pops `ECX` whole — then a match-result/think-byte short-circuit to mode `0x14`, else on `DS_00104B14 == 0` the versus-screen hook `FN_000430E8` (`fight_hook_430e8`, already ported, record §46-B — its own header already named this exact store site) plus a mode-`0x1A` wipe with return mode `0x11`, else mode `0x14` too). The other twelve states (0, 4, 5, 7, 8, 0xB..0xE, primed by 0xF/0x10) gate on three unported functions that this task identifies for the first time as a genuine, separate subsystem: `0x1ECC8`/`0x1EC38` (which, read by hand down to their own callee `0x2DDE4`, turn out to rank `DS_001077EC`/`DS_00107880` — each fighter's post-match score, the per-side record's `+8` field — against a table at `0x2D400`, i.e. a **high-score-table rank lookup**, not a generic timer) and `0x1F458` (2897 B, its own subsystem, bigger than `0x1EEB0` itself — eight of its ten callees are already ported, but its own body is a deeply nested analog/digital-cursor state machine well beyond this task's scope regardless), which turns out to be the **post-match high-score name-entry screen** those ranked states wait on. This is exactly the pairing `docs/PROGRESS.md`'s own cycle-2 paragraph flagged without explanation two entries up in this file's history: "the interactive match ... `0x1EEB0`, `0x1F458` ... remains unowned" — this task supplies the why. Two of the four gated states have a portable short-circuit in the raw's own `AND`-chain, ported here: state 0 only calls `0x1ECC8` when `DS_00104AD4 == 2 && DS_00104B1F == 0` (else `DS_00104B25 = 4` unconditionally); state 4 only calls `0x1EC38` when `DS_00107813 == 0` (else `DS_00104B25 = 7`); state 7 mirrors state 4 on `DS_001078A7` (else `DS_00104B25 = 0xA`). Past that short-circuit, and for states 5/8/0xB..0xE/0xF/0x10 entirely, the state cannot determine whether to advance without the unported gate, so it is left parked with a cited `PORT:` note — exactly the "still waiting" behaviour those states already have while their own poll reports not-done, not a fabricated stub. No new callee ported (every call this function makes was already ported before this task); `flow.c`'s stale comment on `frontend_match_start`'s "unwired" callers is corrected (three of the four are now wired, from states 2/6/9). New `check_mode_1e` in `test_fight.c` (placed last in `test_fight`'s call list, after `check_mode4_spawn_gate`, for the same free-list-accumulation reason that function is last), four parts: `check_mode_1e_parked` proves all nine gated/no-op states leave every seeded field exactly at its sentinel; `check_mode_1e_gates` proves states 0/4/7's short-circuit both ways; `check_mode_1e_0a` proves state 0xA's four exits, the hook checked through `fn_origin(fight_hook_430e8)`; `check_mode_1e_bookkeeping` proves states 2/3/6/9, reusing record §46-A's own "fresh CMOS" `hiscore_init` recipe so `frontend_match_start` runs for real. Two mutations (state 4's short-circuit condition inverted; state 0xA's wipe-arm return mode changed `0x11` → `0x10`) each fail the suite and were reverted. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` is green three times, no SIGBUS. `make verify` (run with worktree-local `*_DUMP` overrides to avoid colliding with sibling sessions' concurrent runs on the shared `/tmp` paths the Makefile hard-codes) is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — expected, since mode `0x1E` is reachable only through mode `0x13`'s challenge poll, itself reachable only through the still-unported match-end stores `0x27DC8` (record §48-K) the no-input demo/attract/front-end paths never reach. Remaining named gaps: the rank probe `0x1ECC8`/`0x1EC38` and its callees `0x2DDE4`/`0x2DB58`; the name-entry driver `0x1F458` and its two still-unported callees `0x1FFD0`/`0x20710`; the `0x2C3FC` voices (§45-A) and `0x2DAE4` (spec §7); and `0x27DC8` itself (record §48-K), as already noted for modes 8/0xA.

**Modes `0x18`/`0x19`'s effects-gated hooks `0x4F6E8`/`0x4F704` (record §49-I).** `game_frame`'s cases `0x18`/`0x19` (jump table `0x24B8C` entries `0x253F5`/`0x253FC`) now run `0x4F6E8`/`0x4F704`, ported as `frontend_mode_18_step`/`frontend_mode_19_step` (`flow.c`). The task's guess that the pair (only `0x1C` bytes apart) might be a small sibling family like the mode `0x15`/`0x16`/`0x17` countdown group (records §48-X/§49-G/§46-G) is confirmed only structurally, not mechanically: both are the same tiny `DS_00104AE4` hook idiom this file has already ported three times (gate, `fn_resolve` hook call, optional mode store) — but the gate here is the effects-in-flight count `DS_0009AF3D` (already named, `effects_active()`'s backing byte), not a `DS_001088EE`/`DS_00104AFE` frame countdown, and neither calls `frontend_skip_check`. `0x4F6E8` (mode `0x18`) stores `DS_00104B00 = DS_00104AFA` after the hook returns, exactly as `0x4F2B0`'s (mode `0x16`) expiry arm does; `0x4F704` (mode `0x19`) omits that store, the same hook-only shape `0x4F318`'s (mode `0x17`) arm has — so the two are two independent one-instruction-shorter/longer leaves sharing a skeleton, not a shared body, and are ported as two separate C functions per this repo's one-function convention. Both are callee-free (`port/decomp/prage.functions.csv`: `callees=0` for both), confirmed against the raw disassembly from the Ghidra HTTP bridge (`0x4F6E8`: `cmp byte [9af3d],0; jnz 4f703; call [104ae4]; mov ax,[104afa]; mov [104b00],ax; ret` — `0x4F704`: the same gate and call, `ret` with no store). New `check_mode_18`/`check_mode_19` in `test_fight.c` (registered right after `check_mode_16`), sharing a new lightweight `m1819_seed` fixture (no `mz_save`/`game_string_table_load` needed, since the registered test hook `0x26978`/`game_hook_26978` — the same one `check_mode_16` already uses — is pool/resource-independent): each proves the gate-closed no-op, the gate-open-with-unregistered-hook case (hook skipped, and only `0x18` still stores the mode), and the gate-open-with-registered-hook case, where the two functions' one real behavioural difference shows up — `check_mode_18` ends with `DS_00104B00`'s low word at the hook's own post-call `DS_00104AFA` (`0x23`, written a second time by `0x18`'s own store), while `check_mode_19` ends with it at `0x1A`, the value `frontend_wipe_arm` (called from inside the hook) leaves as a side effect, since `0x19` issues no store of its own to overwrite it — an initially-wrong test expectation (assumed `0x19`'s case left the mode dword at the pre-hook sentinel) that the raw disassembly corrected. Two single-site mutations (the gate inverted on `0x18`, and `0x18`'s trailing mode store copy-pasted onto `0x19`) each fail the suite (7 and 2 `CHECK_EQ_INT` failures respectively) and were reverted. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` is green three times (plus the 2 mutation-revert runs), no SIGBUS, no hang (both new functions are straight-line, no loop). `make verify` (run with worktree-local `*_DUMP`/`TITLE_PIN_DIR` overrides to avoid colliding with sibling sessions' concurrent runs on the shared `/tmp` paths the Makefile hard-codes) is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — expected, since no code path this port currently reaches stores mode `0x18` or `0x19` into `DS_00104B00`, so the no-input demo/attract/front-end paths never dispatch through either new case. No named gaps remain from this task: both functions are fully ported, callee-free leaves.

**Mode `0x1F`'s frame handler `0x208F8` (record §49-J).** `game_frame`'s case `0x1F` now runs `0x208F8`, ported as `game_mode_1f_step` (`flow.c`), confirmed as case `0x1F` by reading the jump table `0x24B8C` directly (entry `0x1F` is `0x253D2`, `call 0x208F8`) and by `get_xrefs_to` (one caller, that same call site). It is the "winner roar" screen: a 5-state sub-machine on `DS_00104B25` that resets the screen and spawns a fixed backdrop row plus an optional effect (state 0); spawns two actors, a fixed one and a character-indexed one selected by the `DS_00104529` sprite flag's bit 1 (state 1); waits for the first actor's play position to reach a fixed frame, then hands off to mode `0x15` (the generic countdown/skip mode, already ported, record §48-X) for up to `0x4B0` frames with return mode `0x1F` itself (state 2); on return, spawns a third, character-indexed actor and starts the first two actors on fresh anim streams (state 3); and counts the first pair down to death while the third actor's alpha counts up to a clamp, at which point it spawns a character-indexed actor or draws a character-indexed string (again gated on the sprite flag), arms mode `0x15` once more (`0x384` frames) with return mode **`0x1E`**, and resets its own sub-state to 0 (state 4). That `0x1F` → `0x15` → `0x1F` → … → `0x15` → `0x1E` return-mode chase is the same "hand off to the generic countdown mode, come back via `DS_00104AFA`" idiom record §49-H already documented for mode `0x1E`'s own bookkeeping states, and it places mode `0x1F` immediately before `0x1E`'s post-match challenge/high-score flow in the game's own sequencing — matching this task's own working title, "roar-timing". No new callee was needed: every real callee (`frontend_input_reset`, `actors_reset`, `frontend_spawn_row`, `frontend_list_next`, `effects_spawn`, `actor_spawn`, `actors_anim_begin`, `actor_set_dead`, `text_cursor_set`, `game_string_get`) was already ported before this task; one `0x2C3FC` voice call in state 0 is a `PORT:` no-op as usual (record §45-A, spec §7). Building the test fixture surfaced two things worth recording: `frontend_list_next`'s "0x10-stride table" (`DS_00107608..DS_00107798`) turns out to be `palette_acquire`'s own 24-entry palette-handle table (record §47-C), not a free-standing "row" table, so state 0's own `frontend_spawn_row` call (which resolves a real handle for the real `0xA7B80` backdrop descriptor in the `ghidra_data.bin` oracle) always registers a palette entry there before the list-walk runs; and `rec+0x34` (a dword) and `rec+0x36` (a word) used by state 2's threshold check are the *same* storage — the dword-read-then-`SAR 0x10` idiom is exactly `(s16)word[+0x36]` sign-extended, confirmed against `actor_spawn`'s own child-actor arm, which writes both as adjacent word fields. New `check_mode_1f` in `test_fight.c` (`check_mode_1f_state0`..`_state4`, placed last in `test_fight`'s call list, after `check_mode_1e`, for the same free-list-accumulation reason that function is last: a new `m1f_seed` fixture builds a real actor pool from dedicated scratch addresses and calls `actors_reset()` on every seed). Each state's spawns and field transitions are exercised for real (not stubbed), including both `DS_00104529` branches in states 1 and 4, both sides of state 2's threshold (with a non-zero "parked" sentinel so it can't coincide with the advance arm's own zero write), and state 4's countdown-to-exactly-zero kill, alpha clamp and both finalize arms. Three mutations (state 2's threshold direction, state 4's kill-condition equality, state 1's branch-table swap) each fail the suite and were reverted; `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` is green three times (plus the mutation-revert runs), ≈2s each, no SIGBUS. `make verify` (run with worktree-local `*_DUMP`/`TITLE_PIN_DIR` overrides) is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — expected, since mode `0x1F` is reachable only through the still-unported match-end path the no-input demo/attract/front-end paths never reach. Remaining named gap: the one `0x2C3FC` voice (record §45-A); the store that actually sets `DS_00104B00 = 0x1F` was not identified by this task and likely lives in the same match-end/high-score territory as `0x27DC8` (records §49-C/§49-D/§49-H).
**Modes `0x30`/`0x31`/`0x33`, mode `0x32`'s continue/rematch chain (record §49-K).** `game_frame`'s cases `0x30`/`0x31`/`0x33` — until now grouped in the tail named-gap fallthrough list — now run `0x29328`/`0x299E8`/`0x29638`, ported as `game_mode_30_step`/`game_mode_31_step`/`game_mode_33_step` (`flow.c`). All three turn out to be mode `0x32`'s own continue/rematch chain, exactly matching the pairing `flow.h`'s pre-existing header comment on `flow_match_result_text` already named without elaboration: "the arena frame's tail steps, then on `DS_00104B0C` the next opponent (modes 0xC / 0x31) or the match's end (modes 0xF / 0x33)" — modes 0xD/0xC/0xF and modes `0x32`/`0x31`/`0x33` are the same three-step shape twice over, once for the normal round-loss path and once for mode `0x32`'s own rematch path. Mode `0x30` is a fourth family member, but paired with mode 5's `0x25C88` (record §48-U) rather than `0x32`: its states 1 and 4 are byte-identical to mode 5's own (the same round-card descriptor tables, the same win-marker reset, the same countdown-into-next-state handoff), state 2 is almost identical (one voice-id difference, inconsequential since voices are already a named gap, §45-A), and state 3 is the new shape — it only advances the mode word to `0x31` when `DS_00104B21 == 0`; the raw's own `jnz` skips to the shared tail otherwise, leaving both the sub-state and the mode word untouched, so that state's cleanup would repeat every frame if ever entered with `DS_00104B21 != 0` (ported as observed, not believed reachable: only mode `0x32`'s own twin state zeroes that counter, never mode `0x30` itself). Mode `0x31`'s `0x299E8` is mode 0xC's `0x27380` (record §48-C) verbatim for its prelude, gated projection block and tail, plus an unconditional frozen-pose undo at entry (the same 0x1E1-blink-restore idiom `0x27380` itself already runs on the same slot, gated by the mode-0xC tail instead) and `flow_round_over_check` (the small, previously-unported `0x29970`, now ported alongside it) in place of `flow_arena_ko_check` at the tail. `flow_round_over_check` is a two-branch KO-threshold check (each side's `+0x5A` score byte at or above `0x78`) that sets mode `0x32` and the deciding side before calling the already-ported `flow_round_winner`. Mode `0x33`'s `0x29638` is the match's-end wait — the crowd/ambience walker `0x4DEF4` left as a named gap (below) — counting `DS_00104AFE` down from the `0x258` mode `0x32` arms it to, then mode `0x17` with the hook `FN_00025AE8` (already named by `game_hook_25ae8`'s own header as one of its two callers), closing the pairing: mode `0xD`/`0xC`'s exit is mode `0xF` (still a named gap, in scope for the sibling `gap26-modes7f` batch); mode `0x32`'s exit is mode `0x33`, now ported. New tests in `test_fight.c`: `check_mode_30` (five state-1 descriptor combinations checked against the descriptor's own `+4` "type" byte rather than `+0`, since `actor_spawn`'s animation-stream walk mutates `+0` in place post-spawn for some descriptors — a real, already-ported behaviour this test learned the hard way; three state-2 combinations; state 3 both branches, including the "stuck" quirk proven by calling twice; state 4's countdown boundary; five parked states), `check_flow_round_over_check` (both sides, on the existing `k48_seed` fixture), `check_mode_31` (the pset-restore prelude's four combinations; the shared prelude/tail via `check_mode_0c`'s own established `c_seed` fixture; the projection gate; the round-over integration via side 0's threshold only — side 1's threshold, through `c_seed`'s minimal fixture, was found by direct reproduction to drive the already-ported, already-tested `fight_slot_pass` into an unrelated wild-pointer crash inside `hit_connect`'s own combat-descriptor lookup, a pre-existing gap in that function's test coverage unaffected by anything this task changed, worked around by relying on `check_flow_round_over_check`'s symmetric, crash-free coverage of both sides instead of forcing the unsafe combination), and `check_mode_33` (the countdown's signed `<= 0` boundary, including the `0 -> 0xFFFF` wrap). A second live-fixture hazard, also fixed rather than routed around: `flow_win_markers_spawn`'s own pre-existing unbounded loop (its header already says "nothing caps k at the four slots") is now reachable from two new paths (mode `0x30`'s state 1, and `flow_round_over_check` -> `flow_round_winner`), and neither `q_mode_seed` nor `c_seed` — unlike `k48_seed` — zero the two loop-bound bytes `DS_00104AF2`/`DS_00104AF3`; a leftover nonzero byte from a prior test drove a real SIGSEGV before the fix (both seed helpers now zero the two bytes explicitly). Five mutations (state 1's next-state store; `flow_round_over_check`'s threshold operator; mode `0x33`'s expiry mode-word constant; the pset-restore word match) each fail the suite and were reverted. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` green x3 (plus 5 mutation-revert runs), no SIGBUS, no hang. `make verify` (worktree-local `*_DUMP`/`TITLE_PIN_DIR` overrides) is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — expected, since this whole chain is reachable only through mode `0x32`'s own transitions, themselves gated behind the still-unported match-end stores `0x27DC8` (record §48-K), as already noted for modes 8/0xA/0x1E. Remaining named gaps: `0x4DEF4` (shared with mode `0xF`'s still-unported `0x277C0`); `0x27DC8` itself; the `0x2C3FC` voices (§45-A) and `0x32970` (the run clock, spec §7).
**Mode `0x21`'s frame handler `0x26540` (record §49-O).** `game_frame`'s case `0x21` — another entry in the generic named-gap fallthrough list — now runs `0x26540`, ported as `game_mode_21_step` (`flow.c`). The task's own hint speculated it might belong to the same round/match-transition cluster as mode 4's fight frame; the disassembly confirms something stronger: `0x26540` is a byte-for-byte near-duplicate of the fight frame `0x26254` (`game_mode_04_step`, record §48-K) — the identical preamble (`fight_slot_clear`, `camera_screen_base` per side, the two position latches), the identical `DS_001078FA == 2`-gated projection block (`camera_project` per side, `camera_decay`, `fighter_pass_a`, `fighter_pass_b(0)`, `camera_project` per side again, `fighter_think`, `camera_project` per side a third time), then `fight_slot_pass` and `fight_hud_pass` per side, all instruction-for-instruction identical to `0x26254`'s own port. It diverges in exactly one call and one omission: where `0x26254` runs `fight_effects_pass` (`0x49C78`), `0x26540` runs the unported `0x4BF18` — the attract loop's volleyball mini-game's own per-frame driver, already named "the mode-`0x21` pass" by `fight.c`'s pre-existing comments on two of its callees, `fight_4c60c`/`fight_4cc0c` (record §43-A, "demo-pose"), written before this task. `0x4BF18` itself is an ~150-instruction 8-state switch over the effects list with several of its own unported callees (one, `0x4A868`'s case 14, already carried a `PORT:` note in `fight.c` predating this task) and its return value is discarded by the raw, so it stays a named gap, documented at the call site rather than stubbed. The other divergence: `0x26254` closes with a `DS_001078FA`/`DS_00108892`/`DS_00107803` gate into `fight_mode25_enter` (`0x4E11C`); `0x26540` has none — the raw ends at `DS_00104AEC |= 2` and a plain `pop edx; pop ecx; pop ebx; ret`. Porting this surfaced a pre-existing test collision: `test_fight.c` already drove `game_frame()` in mode `0x21` (part F of its `camera_pair_hold`/`0x25414`-family block) specifically *because* case `0x21` was, until now, a documented no-op in the first of `game_frame`'s two mode-keyed switches, letting that test isolate the second switch's own, unrelated, already-ported case `0x21` (`fighter_body_push`/`camera_pair_hold(1)`/`fight_health_bars`, at `0x25509`). Now that the first switch's case `0x21` runs real logic first, its `flow_round_end_check` call could overwrite `DS_00104B00` away from `0x21` before the second switch ran, which is exactly what the first build showed (every one of that test's post-conditions came back as its own pre-call sentinel). Fixed by seeding `DS_0010780A`/`DS_0010789E`/`DS_001088F2` into the shared `MT_SEED` macro so `flow_round_end_check` is a deterministic no-op — harmless for the macro's other modes, none of which reach that call — with every original assertion value left unchanged. New `check_mode_21` in `test_fight.c` (registered right after `check_mode_0a`), reusing `0x26254`'s own `k48_seed` fixture directly (proven applicable since the two functions share the exact preamble/tail shape) with `DS_001078FA` left at 5 (not 2, the no-projection case, matching `0x26254`'s own recorded test values for the same seed): the shared preamble/tail's position latches, `fight_hud_pulse` countdown, `DS_00104AEC |= 2` and the inert round-end check, plus one `0x26540`-specific canary — `DS_00108874` (`fight_effects_pass`'s own first, unconditional write), seeded to a sentinel and proven to move under `game_mode_04_step` from the identical seed (so the canary is live) but stay untouched under `game_mode_21_step`, the direct proof that `0x4BF18`'s gap is skipped rather than silently substituted. A mutation adding a `fight_effects_pass()` call into the gap's position was rebuilt, confirmed to fail the canary, and reverted. `game_mode_21_step` itself has no loop of any kind — a straight-line call sequence with one forward conditional skip, matching the raw's single `JNZ` — so it carries none of the loop-exit-condition risk a sibling task's `run_tests` hang (caught and fixed separately, in `gap26-modes7f`) warned this batch to double-check for. `PR_ORACLE_REQUIRED=1 run_tests` is green, no SIGBUS, no hang. `make verify` (run with a worktree-local `FRONTEND_DUMP` override, `make FRONTEND_DUMP=/tmp/pr_frontend_dump_gap29 verify`, to avoid the shared-`/tmp` collision record §49-H already flagged) is unchanged (front-end 517/801/3/2, demo-fight fully explained at N = 1886, attract2 0 unexplained at N = 3617, `symbols.h` idempotent) — expected, since mode `0x21` is reachable only through the still-unported match-end stores `0x27DC8` (record §48-K), as already noted for modes 8/`0xA`/`0x1E`. Remaining named gaps: `0x4BF18` and its own unported callees; cases `0x22`/`0x23`/`0x24`, still grouped in the generic named-gap list and left untouched (the task's hint that they might be the same tiny stub as `0x21` did not hold for `0x21` itself, which turned out to be `0x26254`'s near-duplicate, not a stub, so checking whether it holds for its neighbours is left for a future task); and `0x27DC8` itself.

**Modes `0x22`/`0x23`/`0x24`'s frame handlers `0x26C8C`/`0x26A50`/`0x26F58` (records §49-L/§49-M/§49-N).** `game_frame`'s cases `0x22`/`0x23`/`0x24` now run `0x26C8C`/`0x26A50`/`0x26F58`, ported as `game_mode_22_step`/`game_mode_23_step`/`game_mode_24_step` (`flow.c`). Modes 0x22 and 0x24 share a per-side camera preamble byte-identical between the two (confirmed by diffing their first 0x88 bytes instruction for instruction): `fight_slot_clear`, `camera_screen_base(side, class)`, a field copy `DS_001077E8[side] = DS_001077E4[side]`, `camera_project` with all six arguments resolved to already-`symbols.h`-named per-side globals, `fight_slot_pass`, `fight_hud_pass(side)`, `fight_4d2d0` (the already-ported effects pass). Mode 0x22 then runs `camera_y_commit`, a gated `camera_impact_dust_spawn` (new, `0x128D4`, when `DS_00104AC4 > 1`) and an unconditional tail `0x26D4C` (new, `flow_26d4c`, `static`); mode 0x24 instead runs a countdown that, on expiry, resets the join/character-select scratch, re-arms `game_hook_27134` (the exact store that hook's own pre-existing header comment already cited as "stored at `0x27074 (0x26F58)`" before this task ported the call site itself) and closes the play-time accounting through the already-ported `config_play_time_close`, with `camera_y_commit` deferred to a single call both paths converge on. `0x128D4` turns out to be `camera_dust_spawn`'s (`0x1282C`, already ported) near-twin: four times as frequent (a `0xF` gate mask, not `0x3F`) and anchored to the fixed word `DS_000BD898` instead of a random draw for one of its three spawn arguments — the raw reaches that fixed value through a genuinely convoluted `mov eax,edx; sub eax,edx` (a literal `EAX = 0`) plus a 4-byte stack read straddling `[esp-2]`, which the port replaces with the direct value it computes to. `0x26D4C` (mode 0x22's tail, spanning right up to `0x26F58`'s own entry, confirmed by both functions' addresses) draws a percentage from a per-side byte (`DS_0010780B[side]`, `* 100 / 0x78`) and either spawns two background actors plus a number readout, or (the raw's own never-ported `sprintf`, `0x65546`) formats and concatenates two strings — the port reuses the `snprintf`-based substitution `game_overlay_step`'s `0x2BF08` and `prompt_insert_coin_blink`'s `0x2C1FA` already established for this codebase, rather than porting the formatter itself. Mode 0x23 is a 4-state sub-machine on `DS_00104B25` (arm the round, spawn a round card, kill it and draw challenge strings, count down), whose state 1 needed a new callee: `0x1DAE8`, initially mistaken for a thin wrapper around the already-ported `fight_hud_spawn`/`fight_hud_spawn_b`, turns out to be a genuinely smaller single-side variant (two of the four HUD spawns, not all four, and a different, previously-unported `0x1DA84` — `fight_hud_bar_set_inv`, `static` — in place of `fight_hud_bar_set` for the bar's sprite index, with the index inverted and no `DS_00104B1D` table switch).
